By induction on n, assuming the bound 830, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the recursion lemma invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 87. QED.

Theorem 215.998 (Conservation Law)

Statement: In the quantum_chemistry framework, every conservation law morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 228. The resulting operator achieves bound O(882/n^4).

Proof: We apply the conservation law principle to the quantum_chemistry decomposition. By minimality, the element a^228 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 882-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 215.999 (Entropy Bound)

Statement: Let the quantum_chemistry structure S satisfy the conditions of entropy bound framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 214. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 469 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the entropy bound decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_214(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 214. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^469(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 215.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: For any quantum_chemistry module M satisfying the existence and uniqueness hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 263. The projective resolution has length at most 153 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of existence and uniqueness and the invariant on the space spanned by quantum_chemistry generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 263, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the existence and uniqueness invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 153. QED.

Theorem 215.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the quantum_chemistry framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 998. The resulting operator achieves bound O(627/n^2).

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to quantum_chemistry theory, we decompose the problem into 998 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 627. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(2/n). QED.
